Auth bypasses are so hot right now.

https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/cve-2026-112352

Sunhillo SureLine before 9.1.5 contains an authentication bypass vulnerability that allows an unauthenticated remote attacker to impersonate any user with an active web session by forging a session cookie containing only a cleartext username with no random identifier or integrity protection. Attackers can guess default account names such as Admin or sftp_user and submit a forged username cookie to satisfy the login check in checkLogin (libwebcmn.so) and networkDiag.cgi, chaining into root command execution.

🚨🇺🇸 Veeam patches critical Backup & Replication authorization bypass

Veeam has released security updates addressing five vulnerabilities across Backup & Replication, Veeam Agent for Linux and Veeam ONE.
⠀
The most serious, CVE-2026-108142, carries a CVSS score of 9.1 and allows an unauthenticated attacker to bypass authorization on the CDP Coordinator Service.
⠀
Other patched vulnerabilities include:

• CVE-2026-105477: Local privilege escalation in managed Veeam Agent for Linux installations, CVSS 7.3
• CVE-2026-108140: Arbitrary file writes through path traversal, CVSS 5.9
• CVE-2026-108141: Arbitrary file writes through path traversal, CVSS 5.9

CVE-2026-114607
(9.1 CRITICAL)

EPSS: 0.42%

updated 2026-10-27T15:32:09

1 posts

## Am I affected? You may be affected if you use `better-auth` from `1.4.0-beta.18` through versions before `1.7.7` with the Magic Link plugin and social or Generic OAuth sign-in. You are affected if **all** of these are true: - Magic Link is enabled with default `storeToken: "plain"`, or with `storeToken: "hashed"` and global `verification.storeIdentifier: "hashed"` (including a hashed default

cR0w@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-27T16:53:11.000Z ##

Better than what?

https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/cve-2026-114607

Better Auth is an authentication and authorization library for TypeScript. From 1.4.0-beta.18 until 1.7.7, deployments that enable Magic Link with storeToken set to plain, or with storeToken set to hashed and the global verification.storeIdentifier setting also set to hashed, enable a built-in social or Generic OAuth provider, and use account.storeStateStrategy set to database can accept an OAuth sign-in state value as a Magic Link token. These settings can cause Magic Link verification records and OAuth state to resolve to the same stored identifier, allowing an unauthenticated attacker to start OAuth sign-in for a known email address and redeem the returned state through Magic Link without controlling the mailbox or completing the provider callback. The attacker can obtain a normal session for an existing user, or create an account incorrectly marked as email verified when sign-up is enabled. This issue is fixed in version 1.7.7.

Apple Patches Exploited CoreGraphics Flaw and Hundreds of Vulnerabilities in October 2026 Security Update

On October 26, 2026, Apple released security updates across iOS, iPadOS, macOS, tvOS, watchOS, visionOS and Safari, patching several hundred vulnerabilities concentrated in WebKit, the kernel, ImageIO and CoreStorage. The fixes include WebKit code-execution and sandbox-escape flaws, a Remote Management remote code execution bug, and many privilege-escalation issues, plus the most urgent one: CVE-2026-86950 in CoreGraphics, which was exploited in targeted attacks and makes updating to iOS 18.7.11 critical for owners of older iPhones.

**If you use any Apple device (iPhone, iPad, Mac, Apple Watch, Apple TV or Vision Pro), update it now. The current versions are iOS/iPadOS 27.2, 26.7.2 or 18.7.11, macOS Golden Gate 27.2, Tahoe 26.7.2 or Sequoia 15.8.2, tvOS/watchOS/visionOS 27.2, and Safari 27.2. If you still use an iPhone XS, XS Max or XR, install iOS 18.7.11 first: it fixes a flaw that has been exploited in targeted attacks. Until you've updated, be extra careful about unfamiliar websites, files, images and fonts from strangers, and connecting to unknown file-sharing or Screen Sharing servers.**

TrustedAlpaca@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-25T22:09:52.000Z ##

This week has been extremely busy. I have seen 4x RCE vulnerabilities, compromised eMudhra CA certificates that spill into their own CVE for Java with a CVSS of 9.1, and 3x DoS type of vulnerabilities, all in very popular and widely used software that can either crash under specific conditions or give remote code execution capabilities to threat actors. The mentioned vulnerabilities are:

PHP RCE: CVE-2026-109618/109619/115560
Gitlab RCE: CVE-2026-110264
Node2Shell \ HTTP2Shell RCE: CVE-2026-111497
ISC Bind 9 RCE: CVE-2026-94300

ISC Bind 9 DoS: CVE-2026-19630/96888/97313
Windows SMB Server DoS: CVE-2026-91403
Unbound DNS-over-QUIC Bug: CVE-2026-97206
Oracle Java (JSSE) containing compromised eMudhra certificates: CVE-2026-91403

Patching all these vulnerabilities on endpoints and servers will take a humungous amount of time and effort, as it can't be done overnight and is heavily dependent on the size of your environment. It's good that we are getting security patches for software that had critical vulnerabilities present for years without anyone discovering them, like in the Gitlab case where the RCE vulnerability went undiscovered for 12 years across all their releases.

What will happen over the coming years is that we will get a massive influx of vulnerabilities just like the one seen this week, but over time it will stabilise more as software becomes more secure, and we will start seeing fewer vulnerabilities overall.

cR0w@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-26T18:51:20.000Z ##

JFC

https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/cve-2026-112346

OpenHands Software Agent SDK before 1.52.0 contains an unauthenticated remote code execution vulnerability where the DockerWorkspace and Apptainer workspace launchers bind the sandbox agent server to all network interfaces (0.0.0.0) with an empty session_api_keys list, disabling authentication by default. Attackers who can reach the published container port can send unauthenticated HTTP and WebSocket requests to execute arbitrary shell commands as root in the sandbox, access the mounted workspace, and read plaintext secrets from the secrets store.

TrustedAlpaca@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-25T22:09:52.000Z ##

This week has been extremely busy. I have seen 4x RCE vulnerabilities, compromised eMudhra CA certificates that spill into their own CVE for Java with a CVSS of 9.1, and 3x DoS type of vulnerabilities, all in very popular and widely used software that can either crash under specific conditions or give remote code execution capabilities to threat actors. The mentioned vulnerabilities are:

PHP RCE: CVE-2026-109618/109619/115560
Gitlab RCE: CVE-2026-110264
Node2Shell \ HTTP2Shell RCE: CVE-2026-111497
ISC Bind 9 RCE: CVE-2026-94300

ISC Bind 9 DoS: CVE-2026-19630/96888/97313
Windows SMB Server DoS: CVE-2026-91403
Unbound DNS-over-QUIC Bug: CVE-2026-97206
Oracle Java (JSSE) containing compromised eMudhra certificates: CVE-2026-91403

Patching all these vulnerabilities on endpoints and servers will take a humungous amount of time and effort, as it can't be done overnight and is heavily dependent on the size of your environment. It's good that we are getting security patches for software that had critical vulnerabilities present for years without anyone discovering them, like in the Gitlab case where the RCE vulnerability went undiscovered for 12 years across all their releases.

What will happen over the coming years is that we will get a massive influx of vulnerabilities just like the one seen this week, but over time it will stabilise more as software becomes more secure, and we will start seeing fewer vulnerabilities overall.

#Gitlab #eMudhra #Node2Shell #CyberSecurity #Vulnerabilities

The branch can then jump to arbitrary other code at a user-controlled offset, allowing a user-controlled Wasm module to escape the sandbox on

beyondmachines1@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-27T10:01:42.000Z ##

Wasmtime Patches Critical Sandbox Escape Flaws in Cranelift Compiler

Wasmtime fixed two critical vulnerabilities (CVE-2026-115889 and CVE-2026-115893) that allow malicious WebAssembly modules to escape their sandboxes and execute arbitrary code on host systems. The flaws affect the Cranelift compiler and require immediate updates to Wasmtime 50.0.0 or other patched versions.

**If you use Wasmtime (directly, inside your own apps, or through tools like Spin), update ASAP to version 36.0.19, 48.0.7, 49.0.4 or 50.0.0, rebuild any applications that include it, and update Spin to 4.2.4. If you're still on the 24.x line, there is no patch, so upgrade to a supported version. If possible don't run WebAssembly code from sources you don't fully trust.**
#cybersecurity #infosec #advisory #vulnerability
https://beyondmachines.net/event_details/wasmtime-patches-critical-sandbox-escape-flaws-in-cranelift-compiler-8-z-h-v-8/gD2P6Ple2L

##
DarkWebInformer@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-26T16:26:22.000Z ##

🚨 Two critical Wasmtime flaws could allow malicious WebAssembly modules to escape the runtime sandbox

Both vulnerabilities affect Cranelift, Wasmtime’s default compiler, and carry CVSS scores of 9.9.
⠀
CVE-2026-115889 affects all processor architectures and can trigger silent compiler errors that generate incorrect machine code.

CVE-2026-115893 affects ARM64 systems and can cause large br_table instructions to jump to the wrong code location.
⠀
Exploitation requires a vulnerable Wasmtime instance to compile an attacker-controlled WebAssembly module. Services that accept and execute untrusted customer modules face the greatest risk.

Fixed versions:

• Wasmtime 36.0.19
• Wasmtime 48.0.7
• Wasmtime 49.0.4
• Wasmtime 50.0.0 or later

No exploitation in the wild has been reported.

Sources: https://github.com/bytecodealliance/wasmtime/security/advisories/GHSA-897f-g5gm-fmxm

https://github.com/bytecodealliance/wasmtime/security/advisories/GHSA-mghf-wjfp-p5xm

Okta Patches Command Injection and Authentication Bypass in Access Gateway

Okta released security updates for Access Gateway to fix a command injection flaw (CVE-2026-115547) and an authentication bypass (CVE-2026-115546) that allow attackers to take full control of the appliance. The updates also address two remote code execution vulnerabilities in the PHP runtime used by the gateway.

**If you run Okta Access Gateway, upgrade it to version 2026.10.2 or later to fix the admin takeover flaws, and install version 2026.44.0 to fix the PHP flaws. Make sure the Admin Console can only be reached from trusted internal IP addresses, and check for any unexpected admin logins or configuration changes.**

##
Ars_Technica___All_content@reader.leeholmes.com at 2026-10-27T20:25:55.000Z ##

Code-execution flaw in UEFI firmware threatens most servers on planet Earth

Defenders across the world face the burden of responding to yet another botched code-execution vulnerability, this time in boot-time firmware used by most of the world’s server vendors and data centers.

The vulnerability, with a severity rating of 9.5 out of 10, resides in the TianoCore EDK II, an open source codebase that hardware makers and cloud providers use to create the UEFI firmware that runs during the boot-up sequence. Last year, a representative of the UEFI Forum called EDK2 the "preeminent solution / implementation of the UEFI specifications." Virtually all UEFI suppliers (sometimes still called IBVs or independent BIOS vendors), including AMI, Insyde, and Phoenix, use the reference code extensively. Project Mu, Microsoft's hardened EDK2 fork, used the code containing the vulnerable component as well.

The vulnerability, tracked as CVE-2026-97631, is a memory-corruption flaw in an ICMP component that attackers can exploit to execute malicious code that runs before the operating system and firmware can implement defenses like Secure Boot, which establishes a chain of trust for all code, including the Trusted Platform Module (TPM), loaded during boot. While final payloads delivered by CVE-2026-97631 have no persistence—meaning they run only once—they can give attackers a wide opening to install other payloads that also run each time early in the boot process.

Read full article

TrustedAlpaca@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-25T22:09:52.000Z ##

This week has been extremely busy. I have seen 4x RCE vulnerabilities, compromised eMudhra CA certificates that spill into their own CVE for Java with a CVSS of 9.1, and 3x DoS type of vulnerabilities, all in very popular and widely used software that can either crash under specific conditions or give remote code execution capabilities to threat actors. The mentioned vulnerabilities are:

PHP RCE: CVE-2026-109618/109619/115560
Gitlab RCE: CVE-2026-110264
Node2Shell \ HTTP2Shell RCE: CVE-2026-111497
ISC Bind 9 RCE: CVE-2026-94300

ISC Bind 9 DoS: CVE-2026-19630/96888/97313
Windows SMB Server DoS: CVE-2026-91403
Unbound DNS-over-QUIC Bug: CVE-2026-97206
Oracle Java (JSSE) containing compromised eMudhra certificates: CVE-2026-91403

Patching all these vulnerabilities on endpoints and servers will take a humungous amount of time and effort, as it can't be done overnight and is heavily dependent on the size of your environment. It's good that we are getting security patches for software that had critical vulnerabilities present for years without anyone discovering them, like in the Gitlab case where the RCE vulnerability went undiscovered for 12 years across all their releases.

What will happen over the coming years is that we will get a massive influx of vulnerabilities just like the one seen this week, but over time it will stabilise more as software becomes more secure, and we will start seeing fewer vulnerabilities overall.

#Gitlab #eMudhra #Node2Shell #CyberSecurity #Vulnerabilities

On Windows, reusing a `SocketAsyncEventArgs` instance across IPv4 and IPv6 datagram sockets can retain an undersized cached native address bu

DarkWebInformer@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-26T16:54:18.000Z ##

🚨 Microsoft issues emergency .NET updates after earlier patches failed to fully fix two vulnerabilities

Microsoft says its October 13 updates did not completely address CVE-2026-102870 and CVE-2026-70197, prompting new out-of-band security releases for .NET 8, 9, 10 and 11.
⠀
CVE-2026-102870 is a high-severity remote code execution flaw in the Blazor WebAssembly debugger.

Attacker-controlled data from a debugged application could escape a generated C# string and execute code on a developer’s computer when they evaluate an expression. The vulnerability has a CVSS score of 8.8 and requires user interaction.
⠀
The emergency updates also address:

• CVE-2026-70197, a SocketAsyncEventArgs memory corruption flaw
• CVE-2026-111497, a Brotli decoder out-of-bounds write
• CVE-2026-114148, a Linux source-build tarball ownership issue
⠀
Microsoft warns that updating the shared .NET runtime alone is not enough to fix the Blazor vulnerability when affected NuGet packages are referenced directly.

Developers should update Microsoft.AspNetCore.Components.WebAssembly.Server and DevServer to patched releases, including version 10.0.14 for .NET 10 projects.

Source: https://devblogs.microsoft.com/dotnet/dotnet-october-2026-oob-security-updates

CVE-2026-80097
(8.6 HIGH)

EPSS: 0.53%

updated 2026-09-08T18:33:55

4 posts

Improper authentication in Microsoft Authenticator allows an unauthorized attacker to elevate privileges locally.

1 repos

https://github.com/Ghost-Bx1/AuthenticatorFlaw-CVE-2026-80097

DarkWebInformer@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-27T17:27:45.000Z ##

🚨🇺🇸 Microsoft Authenticator flaw allowed malicious Android apps to steal work account tokens

Tracked as CVE-2026-80097, the high-severity vulnerability allowed an Android app with no permissions to impersonate trusted Microsoft apps such as Teams and request tokens for a work account signed into Authenticator.
⠀
Researchers demonstrated that the stolen tokens could provide access to:

• Email and the ability to send messages
• Files stored in Microsoft services
• Calendars and Teams chats
• Authentication methods and passkey management
⠀
The flaw existed because Authenticator trusted identity information supplied by the requesting app instead of confirming which app actually made the request.
⠀
Microsoft rates the vulnerability 8.6 and says exploitation requires a malicious app on the device and user interaction during authentication. However, researchers reported obtaining tokens without further interaction when a work account was already signed in.
⠀
Microsoft says it has found no evidence of exploitation. The issue affects Authenticator for Android versions before 6.2606.4246.

Users should update to version 6.2606.4246 or later.

Source: https://msrc.microsoft.com/update-guide/vulnerability/CVE-2026-80097

🚨 [CISA-2026:1027] CISA Adds 2 Known Exploited Vulnerabilities to Catalog (https://secdb.nttzen.cloud/security-advisory/detail/CISA-2026:1027)

CISA has added 2 new vulnerabilities to its Known Exploited Vulnerabilities Catalog, based on evidence of active exploitation. These types of vulnerabilities are a frequent attack vector for malicious cyber actors and pose significant risk to the federal enterprise.

⚠️ CVE-2026-111497 (https://secdb.nttzen.cloud/cve/detail/CVE-2026-111497)
- Name: Google Brotli Unchecked Return Value Vulnerability
- Action: Apply mitigations in accordance with vendor instructions, ensuring compliance with CISA’s BOD 26-04 Prioritizing Security Updates Based on Risk (see URL in Notes) guidance and CISA’s “Forensics Triage Requirements” (see URL in Notes). Follow applicable BOD 26-04 guidance for cloud services or discontinue use of the product if mitigations are unavailable. Stakeholders are responsible for evaluating each asset&#39;s internet exposure and ensuring adherence to BOD 26-04 patching guidelines.
- Known To Be Used in Ransomware Campaigns? Unknown
- Vendor: Google
- Product: Brotli
- Notes: This vulnerability affects an open-source component, third-party library, protocol, or proprietary implementation that could be used by different products. For more information, please see: https://github.com/google/brotli/releases/tag/v1.2.1 ; ; BOD 26-04: https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/directives/bod-26-04-prioritizing-security-updates-based-risk ; Forensics Triage Requirements: https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/directives/bod-26-04-implementation-guidance-prioritizing-security-updates-based-risk ; https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-111497

⚠️ CVE-2026-48356 (https://secdb.nttzen.cloud/cve/detail/CVE-2026-48356)
- Name: Adobe Commerce and Magento Unrestricted Upload of File with Dangerous Type Vulnerability
- Action: Apply mitigations in accordance with vendor instructions, ensuring compliance with CISA’s BOD 26-04 Prioritizing Security Updates Based on Risk (see URL in Notes) guidance and CISA’s “Forensics Triage Requirements” (see URL in Notes). Follow applicable BOD 26-04 guidance for cloud services or discontinue use of the product if mitigations are unavailable. Stakeholders are responsible for evaluating each asset&#39;s internet exposure and ensuring adherence to BOD 26-04 patching guidelines.
- Known To Be Used in Ransomware Campaigns? Unknown
- Vendor: Adobe
- Product: Commerce and Magento

shellsharks@shellsharks.social at 2026-10-27T14:11:09.000Z ##

CVE-2026-11497 having a bit of an identity crisis…

- Imperva coined the initial term, “Node2Shell” (https://www.imperva.com/blog/node2shell-from-brotli-data-to-remote-code-execution/)
- Aikido tried to turn it into “HTTP2Shell” (https://www.aikido.dev/blog/python-packages-vulnerable-node2shell-brotli-cve-2026-111497)
- Not satisfied with either of those, someone came up with “Broccoli2Shell” 🥦 (https://broccoli2shell.com)
- And now, super late to the game, humorless Tenable has decided to label it “Brotli2Shell” (https://www.tenable.com/blog/brotli2shell-frequently-asked-questions-for-google-brotli-decoder-cve-2026-111497)

Same vuln, 4 different names (so far). Yeesh…

##
cR0w@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-26T19:13:58.000Z ##

Palo finally published an advisory outlining impact from CVE-2026-111497 in brotli.

https://security.paloaltonetworks.com/CVE-2026-111497

A buffer overflow vulnerability in the Brotli decoder (c/dec/decode.c) in Google Brotli versions 1.0.3 through 1.2.0 on all platforms allows a remote unauthenticated attacker to corrupt adjacent decoder state memory, disclose sensitive memory contents, cause a denial of service, or potentially execute arbitrary code using a crafted Brotli-compressed stream. Users need to update any Brotli libraries or binaries between 1.0.3 through 1.2.0 to the latest available version: 1.0.10 or 1.1.1 or 1.2.1. Brotli is commonly used by many HTTP-based applications including Node.js.

Most importantly:

This issue is applicable to PAN-OS software on PA-Series and VM-Series firewalls that have inspection of Brotli compressed traffic and TLS/SSL decryption enabled on the dataplane. There is no impact on the Management interface (web or API) of the PAN-OS devices.

hacksgr@mastodon.social at 2026-10-26T18:04:02.000Z ##

PHP has rebuilt official Windows packages for PHP 8.2 through 8.5 to fix CVE-2026-111497, a memory corruption vulnerability in the Brotli compression library.

The issue can be reached via the cURL extension when automatic HTTP response decompression is enabled.

Affected packages include versions 8.5.12-1, 8.4.27-1, 8.3.36-1, and 8.2.35-1, incorporating Brotli 1.2.1.

Windows users are advised to update from the official downloads page.

https://en.hacks.gr/nea-paketa-php-gia-ta-windows-diorthonoyn-sovaro-provlima-asfaleias/

##
DarkWebInformer@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-26T17:59:56.000Z ##

🚨 PHP releases new Windows builds to fix critical HTTP2Shell vulnerability

The PHP team has rebuilt its supported Windows packages with Brotli 1.2.1, addressing CVE-2026-111497, an out-of-bounds write flaw that can potentially lead to remote code execution.
⠀
In PHP’s Windows builds, the vulnerable Brotli decoder can be reached through the cURL extension when automatic HTTP response decompression is enabled.

This cURL feature is disabled by default, but applications and HTTP client libraries can turn it on.
⠀
Updated builds:

• PHP 8.5.12-1
• PHP 8.4.27-1
• PHP 8.3.36-1
• PHP 8.2.35-1
⠀
The “-1” suffix indicates that the Windows packages were rebuilt with updated dependencies. The PHP source and runtime version numbers remain unchanged.

This is separate from the PHP2Shell flaws patched on October 21.

Source: https://www.php.net/archive/2026.php

##
DarkWebInformer@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-26T17:19:30.000Z ##

🚨 Crimeflare releases emergency WAF rules for critical Brotli vulnerability

Crimeflare has added two detection rules for CVE-2026-111497 to its Managed Ruleset, targeting Brotli-encoded request bodies and the Next.js image optimization endpoint.
⠀
The vulnerability is an out-of-bounds write in the Brotli decoder. Malformed compressed input can corrupt process memory, potentially causing crashes, memory disclosure or remote code execution.

It affects software that uses vulnerable Brotli versions, including supported Node.js 22.x, 24.x and 26.x releases.
⠀
Crimeflare’s new rules are enabled by default in Log mode. They provide visibility into matching requests but do not block them unless customers change the action.

Crimeflare warns that blocking the Next.js rule may disrupt image delivery and recommends reviewing matches before enabling enforcement.
⠀
Node.js users should update to:

• 22.23.4
• 24.22.1
• 26.12.1

The WAF rules are an additional mitigation, not a replacement for patching.

Source: https://developers.cloudflare.com/changelog/post/2026-10-25-emergency-waf-release

##
DarkWebInformer@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-26T16:54:18.000Z ##

🚨 Microsoft issues emergency .NET updates after earlier patches failed to fully fix two vulnerabilities

Microsoft says its October 13 updates did not completely address CVE-2026-102870 and CVE-2026-70197, prompting new out-of-band security releases for .NET 8, 9, 10 and 11.
⠀
CVE-2026-102870 is a high-severity remote code execution flaw in the Blazor WebAssembly debugger.

Attacker-controlled data from a debugged application could escape a generated C# string and execute code on a developer’s computer when they evaluate an expression. The vulnerability has a CVSS score of 8.8 and requires user interaction.
⠀
The emergency updates also address:

• CVE-2026-70197, a SocketAsyncEventArgs memory corruption flaw
• CVE-2026-111497, a Brotli decoder out-of-bounds write
• CVE-2026-114148, a Linux source-build tarball ownership issue
⠀
Microsoft warns that updating the shared .NET runtime alone is not enough to fix the Blazor vulnerability when affected NuGet packages are referenced directly.

Developers should update Microsoft.AspNetCore.Components.WebAssembly.Server and DevServer to patched releases, including version 10.0.14 for .NET 10 projects.

Source: https://devblogs.microsoft.com/dotnet/dotnet-october-2026-oob-security-updates

##
MissConstrue@mefi.social at 2026-10-26T03:21:11.000Z ##

Oh man, light a candle for #infosec, #sysop, and #ops, cause if you thought the #php #cve was fun, wait till you hear about CVE-2026-111497, AKA as Node2Shell, HTTP2Shell, and Broccoli2Shell, a high-severity out-of-bounds write vulnerability in the Brotli decoder that can permit remote code execution (#RCE). The bug allows data to overwrite memory buffers when a process decompresses untrusted Brotli streams. It was surfaced by #OpenAI’s red team.

Fixed versions were released this weekend, but systems using system-linked Brotli libraries (e.g., Debian, Alpine) must verify their distribution-specific package updates, as the Node.js binary release does not patch those external dependencies.

While researchers from Imperva demonstrated successful code execution on a specific Node.js 24.22.0 Linux arm64 build, no widespread exploitation in the wild has been reported as of October 25, 2026..

https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/cve-2026-111497

##
TrustedAlpaca@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-25T22:09:52.000Z ##

This week has been extremely busy. I have seen 4x RCE vulnerabilities, compromised eMudhra CA certificates that spill into their own CVE for Java with a CVSS of 9.1, and 3x DoS type of vulnerabilities, all in very popular and widely used software that can either crash under specific conditions or give remote code execution capabilities to threat actors. The mentioned vulnerabilities are:

PHP RCE: CVE-2026-109618/109619/115560
Gitlab RCE: CVE-2026-110264
Node2Shell \ HTTP2Shell RCE: CVE-2026-111497
ISC Bind 9 RCE: CVE-2026-94300

ISC Bind 9 DoS: CVE-2026-19630/96888/97313
Windows SMB Server DoS: CVE-2026-91403
Unbound DNS-over-QUIC Bug: CVE-2026-97206
Oracle Java (JSSE) containing compromised eMudhra certificates: CVE-2026-91403

Patching all these vulnerabilities on endpoints and servers will take a humungous amount of time and effort, as it can't be done overnight and is heavily dependent on the size of your environment. It's good that we are getting security patches for software that had critical vulnerabilities present for years without anyone discovering them, like in the Gitlab case where the RCE vulnerability went undiscovered for 12 years across all their releases.

What will happen over the coming years is that we will get a massive influx of vulnerabilities just like the one seen this week, but over time it will stabilise more as software becomes more secure, and we will start seeing fewer vulnerabilities overall.

#Gitlab #eMudhra #Node2Shell #CyberSecurity #Vulnerabilities

DarkWebInformer at 2026-10-27T23:34:45.643Z ##

🚨🇱🇰 Barista Sri Lanka allegedly breached through PHP vulnerabilities, customer information exposed

Barista, one of Sri Lanka's largest café chains with more than 100 outlets, has been targeted by an actor using the handle "secret_solder".

The actor claims to have gained unauthorized server access by exploiting PHP vulnerabilities CVE-2026-109618 and CVE-2026-109619, allegedly achieving remote code execution without authentication.

According to the post, the server was running an outdated PHP 8.1.34 installation with exposed configuration details.

The actor also claims to have extracted customer names, email addresses, phone numbers, and delivery addresses from server memory.

A sample containing customer names and phone numbers was published. The intrusion and vulnerability claims remain independently unverified.

##
DarkWebInformer@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-27T16:29:45.000Z ##

🚨🇺🇸 CISA confirms critical PHP2Shell flaws are now being exploited in attacks

CISA has added CVE-2026-109618 and CVE-2026-109619 to its Known Exploited Vulnerabilities catalog after confirming active exploitation.
⠀
Known collectively as PHP2Shell, the two memory corruption flaws can allow an unauthenticated attacker to remotely execute code on PHP systems serving web requests.

Affected configurations include PHP-FPM, Apache with mod_php, php-cgi, FrankenPHP and LiteSpeed. The PHP team considers mod_php installations at the highest risk.
⠀
Both vulnerabilities have a CVSS 4.0 score of 9.2.

Fixed versions:

• PHP 8.5.12
• PHP 8.4.27
• PHP 8.3.36
• PHP 8.2.35
⠀
CISA has ordered U.S. federal agencies to remediate the vulnerabilities by October 29.

The PHP team says there are no complete mitigations and that updating is the only safe solution.

Source: https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/alerts/2026/10/26/cisa-adds-two-known-exploited-vulnerabilities-catalog

- Name: PHP Memory Corruption Vulnerability
- Action: Apply mitigations in accordance with vendor instructions, ensuring compliance with CISA’s BOD 26-04 Prioritizing Security Updates Based on Risk (see URL in Notes) guidance and CISA’s “Forensics Triage Requirements” (see URL in Notes). Follow applicable BOD 26-04 guidance for cloud services or discontinue use of the product if mitigations are unavailable. Stakeholders are responsible for evaluating each asset&#39;s internet exposure and ensuring adherence to BOD 26-04 patching guidelines.

- Vendor: PHP Group
- Product: PHP

##
TrustedAlpaca@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-25T22:09:52.000Z ##

This week has been extremely busy. I have seen 4x RCE vulnerabilities, compromised eMudhra CA certificates that spill into their own CVE for Java with a CVSS of 9.1, and 3x DoS type of vulnerabilities, all in very popular and widely used software that can either crash under specific conditions or give remote code execution capabilities to threat actors. The mentioned vulnerabilities are:

PHP RCE: CVE-2026-109618/109619/115560
Gitlab RCE: CVE-2026-110264
Node2Shell \ HTTP2Shell RCE: CVE-2026-111497
ISC Bind 9 RCE: CVE-2026-94300

ISC Bind 9 DoS: CVE-2026-19630/96888/97313
Windows SMB Server DoS: CVE-2026-91403
Unbound DNS-over-QUIC Bug: CVE-2026-97206
Oracle Java (JSSE) containing compromised eMudhra certificates: CVE-2026-91403

Patching all these vulnerabilities on endpoints and servers will take a humungous amount of time and effort, as it can't be done overnight and is heavily dependent on the size of your environment. It's good that we are getting security patches for software that had critical vulnerabilities present for years without anyone discovering them, like in the Gitlab case where the RCE vulnerability went undiscovered for 12 years across all their releases.

What will happen over the coming years is that we will get a massive influx of vulnerabilities just like the one seen this week, but over time it will stabilise more as software becomes more secure, and we will start seeing fewer vulnerabilities overall.

#Gitlab #eMudhra #Node2Shell #CyberSecurity #Vulnerabilities

##
TrustedAlpaca@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-25T16:37:24.000Z ##

This week gave me Heartbleed vibes again, a vulnerability that haunted everyone for years. PHP powers 70% to 80% of the internet's websites, including widely popular CMS platforms like WordPress and Drupal. Critical PHP RCE (Remote Code Execution) vulnerabilities, registered under CVE-2026-109618 and CVE-2026-109619 and dubbed "PHP2Shell", were leaked online before the official announcement for security patches took place. In fact, some threat actors attempted to exploit them up to four days before the PHP Foundation officially released patches for PHP versions 5.4 to 8.5.

What will make this situation worse in the coming months and years is the sheer number of legacy systems that cannot be updated. This happens for several reasons: the operating system or dependencies has reached its end of life, the software solution was discontinued and left unmanaged, or the sole person managing a fragile system left the company. Often, because a system still generates high profits, no one has dared to update it in years, leaving it hanging as low-hanging fruit for an easy compromise. Many organisations face a combination of these exact issues and many more.

Furthermore, typical patching cycles require a week or two of piloting to monitor for instabilities or system crashes. Companies are naturally reluctant to skip this testing phase, but the severity of this PHP vulnerability means standard patching cycles must be expedited immediately.

As AI makes waves across every IT and Cyber Security sector, the threat landscape is shifting massively. We are now seeing threat actors use AI to reverse engineer solutions and compromise systems before security patches are even available. Years ago, before AI became a mainstream trend, this level of speed would have been unimaginable. Yet, as with every technological advancement and revolution, here we are.

#php #cybersecurity #patchmanagement #php2shell #wordpress

DarkWebInformer at 2026-10-27T23:34:45.643Z ##

🚨🇱🇰 Barista Sri Lanka allegedly breached through PHP vulnerabilities, customer information exposed

Barista, one of Sri Lanka's largest café chains with more than 100 outlets, has been targeted by an actor using the handle "secret_solder".

The actor claims to have gained unauthorized server access by exploiting PHP vulnerabilities CVE-2026-109618 and CVE-2026-109619, allegedly achieving remote code execution without authentication.

According to the post, the server was running an outdated PHP 8.1.34 installation with exposed configuration details.

The actor also claims to have extracted customer names, email addresses, phone numbers, and delivery addresses from server memory.

A sample containing customer names and phone numbers was published. The intrusion and vulnerability claims remain independently unverified.

##
DarkWebInformer@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-27T16:29:45.000Z ##

🚨🇺🇸 CISA confirms critical PHP2Shell flaws are now being exploited in attacks

CISA has added CVE-2026-109618 and CVE-2026-109619 to its Known Exploited Vulnerabilities catalog after confirming active exploitation.
⠀
Known collectively as PHP2Shell, the two memory corruption flaws can allow an unauthenticated attacker to remotely execute code on PHP systems serving web requests.

Affected configurations include PHP-FPM, Apache with mod_php, php-cgi, FrankenPHP and LiteSpeed. The PHP team considers mod_php installations at the highest risk.
⠀
Both vulnerabilities have a CVSS 4.0 score of 9.2.

Fixed versions:

• PHP 8.5.12
• PHP 8.4.27
• PHP 8.3.36
• PHP 8.2.35
⠀
CISA has ordered U.S. federal agencies to remediate the vulnerabilities by October 29.

The PHP team says there are no complete mitigations and that updating is the only safe solution.

Source: https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/alerts/2026/10/26/cisa-adds-two-known-exploited-vulnerabilities-catalog

🚨 [CISA-2026:1026] CISA Adds 2 Known Exploited Vulnerabilities to Catalog (https://secdb.nttzen.cloud/security-advisory/detail/CISA-2026:1026)

CISA has added 2 new vulnerabilities to its Known Exploited Vulnerabilities Catalog, based on evidence of active exploitation. These types of vulnerabilities are a frequent attack vector for malicious cyber actors and pose significant risk to the federal enterprise.

⚠️ CVE-2026-109618 (https://secdb.nttzen.cloud/cve/detail/CVE-2026-109618)
- Name: PHP Memory Corruption Vulnerability
- Action: Apply mitigations in accordance with vendor instructions, ensuring compliance with CISA’s BOD 26-04 Prioritizing Security Updates Based on Risk (see URL in Notes) guidance and CISA’s “Forensics Triage Requirements” (see URL in Notes). Follow applicable BOD 26-04 guidance for cloud services or discontinue use of the product if mitigations are unavailable. Stakeholders are responsible for evaluating each asset&#39;s internet exposure and ensuring adherence to BOD 26-04 patching guidelines.
- Known To Be Used in Ransomware Campaigns? Unknown
- Vendor: PHP Group
- Product: PHP
- Notes: This vulnerability affects an open-source component, third-party library, protocol, or proprietary implementation that could be used by different products. 🤖 AI generated summary

##
TrustedAlpaca@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-25T16:37:24.000Z ##

This week gave me Heartbleed vibes again, a vulnerability that haunted everyone for years. PHP powers 70% to 80% of the internet's websites, including widely popular CMS platforms like WordPress and Drupal. Critical PHP RCE (Remote Code Execution) vulnerabilities, registered under CVE-2026-109618 and CVE-2026-109619 and dubbed "PHP2Shell", were leaked online before the official announcement for security patches took place. In fact, some threat actors attempted to exploit them up to four days before the PHP Foundation officially released patches for PHP versions 5.4 to 8.5.

What will make this situation worse in the coming months and years is the sheer number of legacy systems that cannot be updated. This happens for several reasons: the operating system or dependencies has reached its end of life, the software solution was discontinued and left unmanaged, or the sole person managing a fragile system left the company. Often, because a system still generates high profits, no one has dared to update it in years, leaving it hanging as low-hanging fruit for an easy compromise. Many organisations face a combination of these exact issues and many more.

Furthermore, typical patching cycles require a week or two of piloting to monitor for instabilities or system crashes. Companies are naturally reluctant to skip this testing phase, but the severity of this PHP vulnerability means standard patching cycles must be expedited immediately.

As AI makes waves across every IT and Cyber Security sector, the threat landscape is shifting massively. We are now seeing threat actors use AI to reverse engineer solutions and compromise systems before security patches are even available. Years ago, before AI became a mainstream trend, this level of speed would have been unimaginable. Yet, as with every technological advancement and revolution, here we are.

#php #cybersecurity #patchmanagement #php2shell #wordpress

CVE-2026-115881
(0 None)

EPSS: 0.00%

MissConstrue@mefi.social at 2026-10-27T19:36:50.000Z ##

Hey gang, the #infosec datascape is on fire again. And do *you* have any #IoT devices?

Critical vulnerabilities were disclosed in the #GoAhead embedded web server, affecting devices from major manufacturers like Palo Alto Networks, #Siemens, and #Boeing. The vulnerability impacts hundreds of millions of IoT, industrial control, and network devices, as many vendors have not yet issued firmware updates to address these specific flaws.

The most severe flaw, CVE-2026-115881 (CVSS 9.1), allows revoked client certificates to bypass security checks in OpenSSL, a critical risk for mutual-TLS (mTLS) configurations.

Also a weak key exchange in the Mbed TLS backend (CVSS 5.9) that can enable passive session decryption, and a certificate chain bypass in LibreSSL builds (CVSS 7.4).

Additionally, a login bypass vulnerability allows authentication with any password if the user's password is stored in the newer Blowfish format.

https://cybersecuritynews.com/goahead-tls-vulnerabilities/

GoAhead embedded web server version 6.1.1 fixes 11 advisories, including a critical OpenSSL revocation check bypass tracked as CVE-2026-115881 and rated 9.1.

The server loaded certificate revocation lists into its default TLS backend but never enabled checking, allowing revoked client certificates to pass login on mutual-TLS setups.

Mbed TLS builds are unaffected.

A LibreSSL legacy verifier issue (CVE-2026-115884) is als…

DarkWebInformer@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-26T16:54:18.000Z ##

🚨 Microsoft issues emergency .NET updates after earlier patches failed to fully fix two vulnerabilities

Microsoft says its October 13 updates did not completely address CVE-2026-102870 and CVE-2026-70197, prompting new out-of-band security releases for .NET 8, 9, 10 and 11.
⠀
CVE-2026-102870 is a high-severity remote code execution flaw in the Blazor WebAssembly debugger.

Attacker-controlled data from a debugged application could escape a generated C# string and execute code on a developer’s computer when they evaluate an expression. The vulnerability has a CVSS score of 8.8 and requires user interaction.
⠀
The emergency updates also address:

• CVE-2026-70197, a SocketAsyncEventArgs memory corruption flaw
• CVE-2026-111497, a Brotli decoder out-of-bounds write
• CVE-2026-114148, a Linux source-build tarball ownership issue
⠀
Microsoft warns that updating the shared .NET runtime alone is not enough to fix the Blazor vulnerability when affected NuGet packages are referenced directly.

Developers should update Microsoft.AspNetCore.Components.WebAssembly.Server and DevServer to patched releases, including version 10.0.14 for .NET 10 projects.

Source: https://devblogs.microsoft.com/dotnet/dotnet-october-2026-oob-security-updates

DarkWebInformer@infosec.exchange at 2026-10-26T00:29:02.000Z ##

🚨 KeePassXC 2.8.0 is out with several security fixes and a major move to Qt 6

Release: https://github.com/keepassxreboot/keepassxc/releases

https://keepassxc.org/download

The update fixes CVE-2026-69150, a use-after-free triggered when importing a crafted legacy KDB file, along with multiple additional browser integration, memory safety, and Windows security issues that are still awaiting CVE assignments.

Security fixes include:

• Crafted KDB import use-after-free
• Browser entry reference injection
• Out-of-bounds writes in native messaging
• Weak Pageant shared-memory mapping names on Windows
• Integer overflow and out-of-bounds read in the legacy KDB importer
• Improved clearing of decrypted database fragments from memory

KeePassXC 2.8.0 also adds remote database synchronization, configurable shortcuts, SSH key generation, Wayland Auto-Type support, Linux Quick Unlock, and native Windows Arm64 builds.
